<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove a power saving for the planar unit-distance problem: for some absolute $`\beta\lt 4/3`$, every set of <i>n</i> points in the Euclidean plane determines $`O(n^\beta)`$ unordered pairs at unit distance.

- **claim-unit-distance-power-saving** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There are absolute constants $0<C<\infty$ and $1\le\beta<4/3$ such that $u(n)\le Cn^\beta$ for every nonnegative integer $n$.
  - Explanation: The maximal number of unordered unit pairs in a planar configuration admits a uniform upper bound with an absolute exponent below the classical incidence exponent. The proof establishes existence without estimating the gap.
- **claim-algebraic-specialization** (construction, [source](#stmt-inc-specialization)): Let $x_1,\ldots,x_n$ be distinct points of $\mathbb R^2$, and let $\mathcal E$ be any collection of pairs $(i,j)$ for which $\lVert x_i-x_j\rVert_2=1$. There are distinct points $x'_1,\ldots,x'_n$ with real algebraic coordinates, arbitrarily close to the respective original points, such that $\lVert x'_i-x'_j\rVert_2=1$ for every $(i,j)\in\mathcal E$.
  - Explanation: A real configuration can be approximated by distinct real algebraic points while preserving all selected unit equations. Additional unit pairs may appear.
- **claim-random-circle-cutting** (construction, [source](#stmt-inc-cutting)): Let $\mathcal P$ be a finite planar point set and let $\mathcal Q$ be a nonempty set of $l$ distinct unit circles. For every $R\ge1$, sample $s=\lceil C R\log(2+l)\rceil$ circles independently and uniformly with replacement, where $C$ is a sufficiently large absolute constant. With probability at least $9/10$, after deleting the points on sampled circles, all remaining points can be assigned to at most $C'(1+s^2)$ open regions, each of which intersects at most $l/R$ of the original circles. Each assigned point belongs to its assigned region.
  - Explanation: Independent circle sampling produces open regions with controlled conflicts after deleting sampled-circle points. The membership assertion handles boundary events explicitly.
- **claim-point-circle-incidence-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-inc-incidence)): For $m$ distinct points and $l$ distinct unit circles in the plane, $$\label{inc:incidence-bound} I\le C\log^2(2+m+l) \bigl(m^{2/3}l^{2/3}+m+l\bigr),$$ where $C$ is an absolute constant. In particular, when $m,l$ are bounded by a fixed power of $t$, the logarithmic factor is $(\log t)^{O(1)}$.
  - Explanation: The point–circle incidence count obeys a logarithmically weakened crossing bound. The proof invokes a stronger incidence estimate and includes empty inputs.
- **claim-expanding-incidence-piece** (construction, [source](#stmt-inc-piece)): From a sequence of $n=t^3$ real planar points with at least $t^{4-o(1)}$ ordered unit pairs, one can obtain real algebraic point and center sets $P,Q$, and a nonempty graph $E\subseteq P\times Q$ consisting of unit incidences, with the following properties. There is a parameter $a=a(t)>0$ such that $a\to0$, $a\log t\to\infty$, and $$\label{inc:scales} \|P\|=t^{1+2a+o(a)},\qquad \|Q\|=t^{2+a+o(a)},\qquad \|E\|=t^{2+2a+o(a)}.$$ Writing $d(p),d(q)$ for graph degrees, uniformly over all vertices, $$\label{inc:degrees} d(p)=t^{1+o(a)},\qquad d(q)=t^{a+o(a)}.$$ The uniform law on $E$ has marginals $\mu(p)=d(p)/\|E\|$ and $\kappa(q)=d(q)/\|E\|$ satisfying $$\label{inc:marginal-atoms} \mu(p)\ge\frac{\gamma}{\|P\|},\qquad \kappa(q)\ge\frac{\gamma}{\|Q\|},\qquad \gamma=\frac{27}{50}=0.54.$$ Set $\eta=\gamma-\tfrac12=1/25$. There is a single sequence $\xi_t\to0$ such that, for every $F\subseteq P$ and $G\subseteq Q$, with counting fractions $u=\|F\|/\|P\|$ and $v=\|G\|/\|Q\|$, $$\label{inc:expansion} \mathbb P_E(p\in F,q\in G) \le \sqrt{uv}\bigl((uv)^\eta+\xi_t\bigr).$$ The sequence $\xi_t$ is independent of the chosen subsets.
  - Explanation: Specialization, cutting, degree trimming and rectangle maximization produce an unbalanced incidence graph with controlled degrees, lower marginal atoms and uniform subset expansion.
- **claim-dominant-matching-label** (bound, [source](#stmt-inc-matching)): For the sequence of edge laws in [inc:piece](#inc:piece), there are absolute constants $s_0>0,C<\infty$ and an index after which the following holds uniformly for every pair of label maps $\ell_P:P\to\mathcal A$, $\ell_Q:Q\to\mathcal A$ into a finite set. If $$s=\mathbb P_E\bigl(\ell_P(p)\ne\ell_Q(q)\bigr)\le s_0,$$ some label $a_0\in\mathcal A$ satisfies $$\mu\{\ell_P\ne a_0\}+\kappa\{\ell_Q\ne a_0\}\le Cs.$$ Moreover, for independent $p,p'\sim\mu$, at every value of $s$, $$\label{inc:point-label-mismatch} \mathbb P\bigl(\ell_P(p)\ne\ell_P(p')\bigr)\le Cs.$$ The analogous statement holds for independent centers of law $\kappa$.
  - Explanation: Small edge-label mismatch forces a dominant label on both marginals with error proportional to the actual mismatch. Independent marginal samples inherit a mismatch bound without an additive expansion error.
- **claim-uniformly-rare-subsets** (bound, [source](#stmt-inc-rare)): Let $\delta_t\to0$ be any nonnegative sequence. The estimates below hold uniformly over all indicated subsets, with error factors depending only on $\delta_t,\xi_t$ and the fixed constants in <a href="#inc:marginal-expansion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="inc:marginal-expansion">[inc:marginal-expansion]</a>:* 1. *If $F\subseteq P$, $G\subseteq Q$, and $h=\mu(F)\le\delta_t$, while $k=\kappa(G)$ is arbitrary, then $$\label{inc:one-small-set} \mathbb P_E(F,G)\le C(\delta_t^\eta+\xi_t)\sqrt{hk} =o(1)\sqrt{hk}.$$ The same assertion holds with the sides interchanged.* 2. *If also $k\le\delta_t$, then $$\label{inc:joint-rare-edge} \mathbb P_E(F,G)\le\tfrac C2(\delta_t^{2\eta}+\xi_t)(h+k) =o(1)(h+k).$$* 3. *If $F\subseteq P$ and $h=\mu(F)\le\delta_t$, then under the wedge law, $$\label{inc:joint-rare-wedge} \mathbb P(p\in F,r\in F)=o(1)h.$$* *In particular, the error factors may be taken outside sums or integrals over any family of sets whose marginal masses obey the same uniform bound $\delta_t$.
  - Explanation: Expansion suppresses joint rare events along edges and wedges uniformly over the exceptional subsets. Uniformity permits the error factors to pass outside level integrals.
- **claim-wedge-information** (bound, [source](#stmt-inc-entropy)): For the wedge law <a href="#inc:wedge-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="inc:wedge-law">[inc:wedge-law]</a>, let $d_{\min,Q}=\min_{q\in Q}d(q)$. If $p\ne r$, then $$\label{inc:wedge-atoms} \mathbb P(p,r)\le\frac{2}{\|E\|d_{\min,Q}} =t^{-2-3a+o(a)}.$$ The diagonal probability is $$\label{inc:wedge-diagonal} \mathbb P(p=r)=\frac{\|Q\|}{\|E\|}=t^{-a+o(a)}=o(1).$$ The mutual information of the point endpoints satisfies $$\label{inc:information-bound} D:=\mathop{\mathrm{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}}\bigl(\mathcal L(p,r)\,\Vert\,\mu\otimes\mu\bigr) =o(\log t).$$
  - Explanation: The geometric codegree bound controls off-diagonal wedge atoms. Together with degree estimates and a vanishing diagonal mass, this leaves little mutual information between point endpoints.
- **claim-short-history-prediction** (bound, [source](#stmt-pred-device)): Let $\omega,\pi,D$ be as in <a href="#pred:wedge-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pred:wedge-law">[pred:wedge-law]</a>–<a href="#pred:information" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pred:information">[pred:information]</a>, let $K,M\ge1$ be integers, and let $T_1,\ldots,T_K$ be independent tests with the same law as $T$. Each test specifies, in addition to $c_T$, a feature map $f_T:[M]\to\mathbb R^d$ and a coefficient map $g_T:Q\to\mathbb R^d$, for an integer $d\ge1$ and constants $B,B'\ge0$, satisfying $$\lVert f_T(c)\rVert\le B,\qquad \lVert g_T(q)\rVert\le B'$$ for all codes, centers and tests. Choose $J$ uniformly from $[K]$, independently of all tests, and retain only the strict prefix $$H(p)=(c_{T_1}(p),\ldots,c_{T_{J-1}}(p)).$$ Conditioned on $J,T_1,\ldots,T_{J-1}$, let $T$ be a fresh test and define $$m_{T,h}=\sum_{x\in P}\mu_h(x)f_T(c_T(x)).$$ Then $$\label{pred:device-bound} \mathbb E_{J,T_1,\ldots,T_{J-1},T} \left\|\mathbb E_{(p,q)\sim\omega} \langle f_T(c_T(p))-m_{T,H(p)},g_T(q)\rangle\right\| \le4BB'\left(\frac DK\right)^{1/4}.$$ Every realized history has at most $M^{K-1}$ values. In particular, a history can be fixed before drawing the fresh test so that its average absolute discrepancy in <a href="#pred:device-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pred:device-bound">[pred:device-bound]</a> is at most the right side.
  - Explanation: The entropy chain rule bounds the dependence remaining at an averaged transcript step. Center-conditioned independence converts that bound into a discrepancy estimate against bounded center features, while the strict prefix leaves the next test fresh.
- **claim-finite-code-acceptance** (bound, [source](#stmt-pred-acceptance)): In the setting of [pred:device](#pred:device), suppose each test specifies a set $\mathcal A_T(q)\subseteq[M]$ of accepted codes for every $q\in Q$. Put $$s_0=\mathbb E_T\mathbb P_{(p,q)\sim\omega} \{c_T(p)\notin\mathcal A_T(q)\}.$$ There is a fixed strict-prefix history $H$, with at most $M^{K-1}$ values, such that $$\label{pred:acceptance-bound} \mathbb E_T\sum_{p,q}\omega(p,q) \sum_x\mu_{H(p)}(x) \mathbf1_{\{c_T(x)\notin\mathcal A_T(q)\}} \le s_0+4\sqrt M(D/K)^{1/4}.$$ The fresh test $T$ is independent of the fixed history construction.
  - Explanation: Indicator features convert the prediction discrepancy into an acceptance-failure bound. A fixed history works before the independent fresh test is drawn.
- **claim-unhashed-strict-cross-prediction** (bound, [source](#stmt-pred-cross)): Let $\omega,\pi,D$ be as above, let $K\ge1,L\ge2$ be integers, and let $T$ be an independent random test giving the state maps just described. Set $$s_0=\mathbb E_T\mathbb P_{(p,q)\sim\omega} \{y_T(p)\notin R_{y_T(q)}\}.$$ There exists a fixed history $H:P\to\mathcal H$, with $\lvert H(P)\rvert\le L^{2K}$, for which the laws of the *true*, unhashed states $$\nu_{h,T}=(y_T)_*\mu_h$$ satisfy $$\label{pred:cross-bound} \mathbb E_T\mathbb E_{(p,q)\sim\omega} [1-\nu_{H(p),T}(R_{y_T(q)})] \le s_0+\frac4L+18(D/K)^{1/4}.$$ The history is formed from earlier tests and does not use $T$.
  - Explanation: Independent coordinate hashing gives bounded feature norms even when the true label set is large. Fresh-hash collision estimates then transfer the prediction bound back to true states.
- **claim-weighted-product-formula** (construction, [source](#stmt-ht-product)): There is a countable collection of absolute-value entries $v$ on $\mathcal N$, each of weight $1/d_{\mathcal N}$, with the following properties. Its archimedean entries are $\|u\|_v=\|\sigma(u)\|$ for the $d_{\mathcal N}$ embeddings $\sigma:\mathcal N\to\mathbb C$, so their total weight is one. All other entries are nonarchimedean. For each nonzero $u\in\mathcal N$, only finitely many nonarchimedean entries have $\|u\|_v\ne1$, and $$\label{ht:product-formula} \sum_v^*\log\|u\|_v=0.$$ Here and below a star denotes the sum with the stated weights.
  - Explanation: Repeated finite-place absolute values encode local degrees using equal weights. Counting complex embeddings separately yields total archimedean weight one and the normalized product formula.
- **claim-height-identities-and-tails** (bound, [source](#stmt-ht-height)): The value $h(u)$ is independent of the chosen finite normal field and of the extensions of the prime absolute values in [ht:product](#ht:product). For $u\in\mathcal N^\times$, $$\label{ht:height-inversion} h(u^{-1})=h(u),\qquad \sum_v^*\|\log\|u\|_v\|=2h(u).$$ For arbitrary $u,v\in\mathcal N$, $$\label{ht:height-inequalities} h(uv)\le h(u)+h(v),\qquad h(u+v)\le h(u)+h(v)+\log2.$$ If a random $X\in\mathcal N$ is independent of a uniformly sampled embedding $\sigma:\mathcal N\to\mathbb C$, then, for $B>1$ and $0<r<1$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb P\{\|\sigma(X)\|>B\}&\le\frac{\mathbb Eh(X)}{\log B}, \label{ht:large-tail}\\ \mathbb P\{X\ne0,\ \|\sigma(X)\|<r\} &\le\frac{\mathbb Eh(X)}{\log(1/r)}. \label{ht:small-tail} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Normalized place weights make height independent of the containing field. The product formula gives inversion symmetry, triangle inequalities give height bounds, and independent embedding sampling gives tail estimates.
- **claim-edge-to-point-height-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-ht-transfer)): Suppose a probability law $\omega$ on two finite vertex sets $P\times Q$, with point marginal $\mu$, has the following property: for all assignments of finite labels to its vertices, two independent $\mu$-points disagree with probability at most $C_0$ times the edge disagreement probability. For any coordinate $U:P\cup Q\to\mathcal N$, $$\mathbb E_{p,p'\sim\mu}h(U_p-U_{p'}) \le C_1\bigl(\mathbb E_{(p,q)\sim\omega}h(U_p-U_q)+1\bigr),$$ where $C_1$ depends only on $C_0$.
  - Explanation: Coordinate partitions turn height tails into label disagreements. The assumed label comparison therefore transfers mean edge-difference height to mean point-difference height.
- **claim-unbounded-height-scale** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ht-bounded)): For a sequence of the incidence pieces in [inc:piece](#inc:piece), with the level spaces and quantities $S$ in <a href="#ht:levels" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ht:levels">[ht:levels]</a>, no subsequence has $S$ bounded. Consequently $S\to\infty$.
  - Explanation: Bounded scale would yield bounded normalized coordinate heights. Prediction at an independent embedding would then approximately satisfy separated neighbor equations at a distinct point, contradicting the determinant estimate.
- **claim-coordinate-state-consistency** (construction, [source](#stmt-ht-states)): The fractional shifts can be fixed so that $$\label{ht:inconsistency-budget} \int m=\mathbb E_E m_e=O(1),$$ with an absolute constant. At each nonarchimedean entry every edge is consistent outside finitely many threshold levels. If two vertices have matching $A$-labels, then $$\|z_x-z_{x'}\|_v\le c_v e^{-s};$$ matching $B_1$-labels similarly implies $\|w_x-w_{x'}\|_v\le c_v e^s$, where $c_v=1$ at nonarchimedean entries and $c_v=\sqrt2$ at archimedean entries. All the level functions can be chosen measurable.
  - Explanation: Random shifts at archimedean places can be fixed with bounded integrated inconsistency. Nonarchimedean coordinate classes are consistent away from threshold equalities, and matching labels imply coordinate closeness.
- **claim-slow-parameter-hierarchy** (construction, [source](#stmt-bud-parameters)): There are integers $K,L,k\to\infty$ such that $$\begin{aligned} K\log L&=o(\log t),& D/K&=o(1),\label{bud:KL}\\ kK\log L&=o(\log t),& k^{20}\left((D/K)^{1/4}+L^{-1}+S^{-1}+d_{Q,\min}^{-1}\right)&=o(1). \label{bud:hierarchy} \end{aligned}$$ Set $\epsilon=k^{-1/16}$ and $e_t=(D/K)^{1/4}+L^{-1}$. Then $k^3/S=o(1)$, $k^{3+1/16}e_t=o(1)$, and $k=t^{o(1)}$.
  - Explanation: History parameters are chosen first, followed by a sufficiently slow sample-count parameter. This separates prediction error, template growth, scale growth and collision costs.
- **claim-rare-exception-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-bud-rare-moments)): Uniformly on $\mathcal G$, $$\label{bud:exception-comparison} h_P+h_Q\le C(f+m)=o(1),\qquad f\le h_P+h_Q+m.$$ Consequently $$\label{bud:first-moments} \mathbb E_\mu H_p^*+\mathbb E_\kappa H_q^*=O(S).$$ Furthermore, with $(p,q,r)$ the ordinary wedge law, $$\label{bud:joint-exceptions} \int_{\mathcal G}\mathbb P_E(I_p=I_q=1)=o(S),\qquad \int_{\mathcal G}\mathbb P(I_p=I_r=1)=o(S).$$
  - Explanation: A dominant coordinate label controls rare-level marginal exceptions. Uniform rare-set estimates make simultaneous edge and wedge exceptions negligible after integration.
- **claim-star-marginals-and-collisions** (bound, [source](#stmt-bud-pair-laws)): Each edge slot of each experiment, after averaging its base, has the original uniform law on $E$. For every $p\in P$, the conditional probability of $q=q'$ in the $P\theta$ experiment, averaged over $\theta$ conditional on $p$, is at most $M/d(p)$.
  - Explanation: All star slots retain the original edge marginal after averaging their bases. The template-conditioned collision bound holds pointwise in the point base, which is needed when exception weights are unbounded.
- **claim-central-window-envelope** (bound, [source](#stmt-bud-intervals)): Let $b\le b'$, $T\ge0$, and $J$ be an interval of finite length, with any choice of included endpoints. Put $I_-=[b-T,b]$, $I_+=[b',b'+T]$, and $a=\|J\cap I_-\|+\|J\cap I_+\|$, where $\|\cdot\|$ denotes length. For $0<\lambda<1$, $$\label{bud:central-envelope} \big[(1-\lambda)2T+2\lambda a-2T\big]_+ \le\lambda\|J\|\, \mathbf1_{\{b,b'\in\operatorname{int}J\}}.$$ If $X$ has any probability law on $\mathbb R$, and $\|\mathbb P(X>s)-1/2\|\le\delta$ throughout $J$, then $$\label{bud:interior-mass} \mathbb P(X\in\operatorname{int}J)\le2\delta.$$
  - Explanation: Excess reward requires overlap with both flanking intervals and hence interior thresholds. A nearly balanced survival function also limits the probability mass strictly inside the central interval, including laws with endpoint atoms.
- **claim-upper-star-score-budget** (bound, [source](#stmt-bud-upper)): For each $i\in\{Q,P,P\theta\}$, $$\label{bud:upper-equation} D_i\le o(S)+10\delta^2\lambda C_i.$$
  - Explanation: For distinct opposite endpoints the product formula cancels the logarithmic score budget. The central-window estimate bounds excess rewards, while degree and template bounds separately control diagonal pairs.
- **claim-lower-rare-star-budget** (bound, [source](#stmt-bud-rare-lower)): For each of the three pair experiments, let $x$ be its star vertex and $y$ an opposite endpoint. Its score integrated outside $\mathcal C$ is at least $$\label{bud:rare-lower-equation} 2\mathbb EH_x^*-2\mathbb EH_y^*-o(S),$$ where both expectations use the original edge marginals on the appropriate sides. In particular this is $2\mathbb E_\kappa H_q^*-2\mathbb E_\mu H_p^*-o(S)$ for $Q$, and its opposite main term for either $P$ or $P\theta$.
  - Explanation: An exceptional base with normal opposite endpoints earns a rare-level reward. Joint exceptions and inconsistency produce negligible integrated losses, leaving opposite marginal exception terms that cancel between orientations.
- **claim-strict-cross-double-sampling** (bound, [source](#stmt-com-ghost)): Let $\nu$ be a probability measure on a finite product set and let $X_1,\ldots,X_k$ be independent samples from $\nu$. For $0<\alpha<1$ and $k\alpha\ge8$, $$\mathbb P\bigl(\exists y:\nu(R_y)<1-\alpha, \ X_1,\ldots,X_k\in R_y\bigr) \le 2(2k+1)^2\,2^{-k\alpha/2}.$$ Consequently, when $k$ is sufficiently large, the templates in <a href="#com:candidates" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="com:candidates">[com:candidates]</a> satisfy $$\label{com:mixture} \mathbb P\bigl(\exists y\in\mathcal Y_\theta: \widetilde\nu_q(R_y)<1-k^{-1/4}\mid q\bigr) \le 4(2k+1)^2\,2^{-k^{3/4}/2}$$ at each level, uniformly in $q$.
  - Explanation: A ghost sample and a finite trace count bound the chance that a low-mass strict cross contains the entire sample. Synthetic state samples extend the estimate to candidates selected from neighbor-history templates.
- **claim-restricted-support-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-com-transfer)): Fix a level and the joint edge–template law $$\mathbb P(p,q,\theta)=\mathbb P_E(p,q)\mathbb P(\theta\mid q).$$ Let $0<\eta<1/2$. As a function of $(q,\theta)$, select a support $U(q,\theta)$ that is a row, a column, or a set of at most two states. On an event $G$ determined by $(q,\theta)$, suppose $$\widetilde\nu_q(U(q,\theta))\ge1-3\eta^2.$$ Then the actual point state $X(p)$ satisfies $$\mathbb P\bigl(G,\ X(p)\notin U(q,\theta)\bigr)\le8\eta.$$ The support type and its coordinates may depend on both $q$ and $\theta$.
  - Explanation: Concentration of the prediction mixture transfers to the actual point only for rows, columns and supports of at most two states. History-defined exceptional sets avoid conditioning the actual state on a adaptively chosen support.
- **claim-common-variation-suppression** (bound, [source](#stmt-com-variation)): For the center-star and point–template-star experiments of [bud:pair-laws](#bud:pair-laws), $$\label{com:variation-eq} C_Q+C_{P\theta}=o(S).$$ Moreover their rare exception means satisfy $$\label{com:center-rare} \mathbb E_\kappa H_q^*\le\mathbb E_\mu H_p^*+o(S).$$
  - Explanation: Strict-cross classification gives a positive combined common-level score under actual template slice weights. Comparing upper and lower budgets suppresses common variation and bounds center exceptions by point exceptions.
- **claim-two-prediction-score-bound** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-com-prediction-score)): If $\mathbb E_\mu H_p^*=o(S)$, then $T\le o(S)$.
  - Explanation: A fixed history-conditioned coupling preserves both point marginals across all levels. The product formula bounds distinct-pair coordinate matching, and the small collision probability controls the diagonal contribution.
- **claim-positive-rare-point-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-com-rare-positive)): The good rare point exceptions satisfy $$\label{com:rare-positive-eq} \liminf_{t\to\infty}\frac{\mathbb E_\mu H_p^*}{S}>0.$$
  - Explanation: Vanishing rare point mass would force vanishing center mass and ordinary point-star variation. Expansion would then make edge bits almost constant throughout the level space, contradicting its defining minority mass.
- **claim-pointwise-rare-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-ext-pointwise)): For every $p\in P$ and every pair of distinct neighbors $q,q'\in Q$, $$\label{ext:pointwise-bound} 2H_p^*\le 3\bigl(H_q^*+H_{q'}^*+m_{pq}+m_{pq'}\bigr) +W_c+W_b+2\log 2.$$
  - Explanation: The product formula is applied separately to each distinct neighbor pair. Neighbor exceptions and edge inconsistency directly charge the point exception contribution, producing a pointwise bound suitable for tail analysis.
- **claim-rare-point-tail-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-ext-tail)): Let $R_0=\epsilon^{-2}=k^{1/8}$. Then $$\label{ext:tail-bound} \mathbb E_\mu\bigl[H_p^*\mathbf1_{\{H_p^*>R_0S\}}\bigr]=o(S).$$
  - Explanation: Capping neighbor heights and integrating expansion over height thresholds controls the tail first moment. Exception mass cannot be concentrated entirely on points of extremely large exception weight.
- **claim-wedge-unary-profiles** (bound, [source](#stmt-ext-profiles)): For an ordinary wedge $(p,q,r)$, there are nonnegative random variables $E_1,E_2$, with $\mathbb EE_1+\mathbb EE_2=o(S)$, such that $$\begin{aligned} \lVert (n_{pq}-n_{rq})-(a_p-a_r)\rVert_1&\le E_1, \label{ext:profile-error}\\ \lVert n_{pq}-n_{rq}\rVert_1&\ge H_p^*+H_r^*-E_2. \label{ext:profile-size} \end{aligned}$$ Here $n_{pq}(v)=-\log\lvert z_p-z_q\rvert_v$.
  - Explanation: On good rare levels the center exception cancels between the wedge edges. Unary endpoint profiles approximate their logarithmic jump difference, while joint exceptions account for negligible errors in both approximation and size.
- **claim-accurate-height-band** (construction, [source](#stmt-ext-band)): There are a constant $c>0$, independent of $t$ along the fixed sequence, a sequence $\delta_t\to0$, and scales $J\to\infty$ such that a set of ordinary wedges of probability at least $$\label{ext:band-mass} \frac{c}{R_0\log(2R_0)}=t^{-o(1)}$$ satisfies, uniformly on the set, $$\label{ext:band-conditions} J\le H_p^*+H_r^*\le2J,\qquad E_1+E_2\le\delta_t(H_p^*+H_r^*).$$ Every such wedge has $p\ne r$.
  - Explanation: Errors are discarded in height-weighted measure before choosing a common dyadic band. Tail control preserves positive weighted mass and yields a substantial set of uniformly accurate off-diagonal wedges.
- **claim-popular-circle-deletion** (bound, [source](#stmt-ext-circles)): Call a real unit circle *popular* if it contains at least $t^{9/10}$ points of $P$. The ordinary wedge probability of $p,r$ lying on a common popular circle is at most $$\label{ext:circle-cost} t^{-3/5+o(1)}+t^{-9/5+o(1)}+\frac{2}{\min_{q\in Q}d(q)}.$$ This is $o(1/(R_0\log(2R_0)))$. In particular, removing such wedges from the set in [ext:band](#ext:band) preserves the lower probability bound <a href="#ext:band-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:band-mass">[ext:band-mass]</a>, after decreasing its constant.
  - Explanation: Pair counting controls the number of popular circles and their multiple intersections. The original wedge law then bounds all remaining bad pairs, including circles whose centers are absent from the incidence graph.
- **claim-organized-pair-graph** (construction, [source](#stmt-ext-pairs)): For the incidence graphs and levels constructed above, there are $J\to\infty$, a sequence $\omega_t\to0$, and a simple bipartite graph $\mathcal E$ between two copies of $P$, each of size at most $t^{1+o(1)}$, with at least $t^{2-o(1)}$ edges, having the following properties. Each edge $(p,r)$ has distinct endpoints and an assigned center $q=q(p,r)\in Q$ with $pq,rq\in E$. Set $$\lambda_{pr}=\frac{z_p-z_{q(p,r)}}{z_r-z_{q(p,r)}}.$$ No edge has both endpoints on any real unit circle containing at least $t^{9/10}$ points of $P$. Uniformly over the edges and complete rectangles of $\mathcal E$, $$\begin{aligned} (z_p-z_r)(w_p-w_r)&=2-\lambda_{pr}-\lambda_{pr}^{-1}, \label{ext:pair-identity}\\ h(\lambda_{pr})&\ge J/3, \label{ext:pair-height}\\ h\!\left(\frac{\lambda_{pr}\lambda_{p'r'}} {\lambda_{pr'}\lambda_{p'r}}\right) &\le\omega_tJ. \label{ext:rectangle-height} \end{aligned}$$ In the last line all four indicated edges are required to be present; their assigned centers need not agree. The edge ratios $\lambda_{pr}$ are unrelated to the fixed score constant $\lambda$.
  - Explanation: Choose a surviving center for each distinct endpoint pair. Large individual ratio height follows from the accurate band; unary profile cancellation makes every complete rectangle product have negligible height.
- **claim-multiplicative-rectangle-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-obstruction)): There is no sequence with the following properties. At index $n$, let $t_n\to\infty$, let $J_n\to\infty$, and let $P_n\subset\mathbb R^2$ be a finite set of algebraic points with $\lvert P_n\rvert\le t_n^{1+o(1)}$. Let $G_n\subset P_n\times P_n$ be a bipartite graph, using two copies of $P_n$, with at least $t_n^{2-o(1)}$ edges. To each edge $(p,r)$ assign a nonzero algebraic number $\lambda_{pr}$. Suppose that, for an absolute $c>0$, $$\begin{aligned} (z_p-z_r)(w_p-w_r) &=2-\lambda_{pr}-\lambda_{pr}^{-1}, \label{alg:distance}\\ h(\lambda_{pr})&\ge cJ_n, \label{alg:large-height}\\ h\left(\frac{\lambda_{pr}\lambda_{p'r'}} {\lambda_{pr'}\lambda_{p'r}}\right)&=o(J_n) \label{alg:rectangle} \end{aligned}$$ uniformly over edges and complete rectangles, respectively. Finally, suppose that no edge has both endpoints on a unit circle containing at least $t_n^{9/10}$ points of $P_n$. Here $h$ is the absolute height from [ht:height](#ht:height).
  - Explanation: A dense sequence of algebraic pair graphs cannot simultaneously have the distance identity, large individual ratio heights, negligible rectangle heights and the popular-circle exclusion. Generic grids, differentiation and private radical valuations yield the contradiction.
- **claim-negligible-height-subfield** (classification, [source](#stmt-alg-small-height-field)): The set $k_0$ is a subfield of $F$, and every element of $F$ algebraic over $k_0$ belongs to $k_0$.
  - Explanation: The negligible-height elements form a relatively algebraically closed subfield of the sequence field. This is relative algebraic closure inside that field, rather than a claim that the subfield is algebraically closed.
- **claim-generic-complete-grid** (construction, [source](#stmt-alg-generic-grid)): For every fixed $N,j\ge1$, one can choose a complete $N$-by-$j$ grid in the pruned graphs on a large set of indices so that its limiting vertices $p_1,\ldots,p_N\in V_1$ and $r_1,\ldots,r_j\in V_2$ have the following property. Select both coordinates of each vertex on a plane locus and its $z$-coordinate on a curve locus. All the resulting $Nd_1+jd_2$ coordinates are algebraically independent over $B_0$. The other coordinates on a curve are algebraic over these coordinates and $B_0$. In particular, $$\label{alg:joint-degree} \operatorname{trdeg}_{B_0(r_1,\ldots,r_j)} B_0(r_1,\ldots,r_j,p_1,\ldots,p_N)=Nd_1.$$ The selected coordinates and edge ratios belong to $F$.
  - Explanation: Minimal-dimensional loci give uniform polynomial avoidance. Complete-grid counting and avoidance of successively longer finite lists of polynomial tests produce algebraically independent limiting coordinates over the locus coefficient field.
- **claim-nonvanishing-field-derivation** (construction, [source](#stmt-alg-derivation)): There is a $k_0$-derivation $\partial:F\to F$ such that $\partial\lambda_{i\ell}\ne0$ for every grid edge.
  - Explanation: Each transcendental edge ratio admits a witness derivation over the negligible-height subfield. A finite linear combination avoids the kernels for all chosen ratios.
- **claim-surviving-generic-point** (construction, [source](#stmt-alg-surviving-point)): Some $p=p_i$ has transcendence degree $d_1$ over $K_0$, and evaluation identifies the function field $K_0(V_1)$ with $K_0(p)$.
  - Explanation: Adjoining right-side positions and derivative data cannot consume all left-side transcendence degrees. A surviving point remains generic over the enlarged coefficient field and identifies its locus function field by evaluation.
- **claim-private-radicand-valuations** (construction, [source](#stmt-alg-private-valuations)): For every $\ell\in\{1,\ldots,j\}$ there is an integer-valued valuation $v_\ell$ of $M$ such that $$v_\ell(D_\ell)=1,\qquad v_\ell(D_m)=0\quad(m\ne\ell).$$ Consequently the classes of $D_1,\ldots,D_j$ are independent in $M^\times/(M^\times)^2$.
  - Explanation: A private simple zero isolates each radicand in the enlarged function field. Its odd valuation prevents any nonempty product of the radicands from being a square, enabling independent radical sign changes.

          "exact_quote": "For a finite set $X\\subset\\mathbb R^2$, write $$u(X)=\\#\\bigl\\{\\{x,y\\}\\subset X:\\ \\lVert x-y\\rVert_2=1\\bigr\\},\n \\qquad\n u(n)=\\max_{\\substack{X\\subset\\mathbb R^2\\\\ |X|=n}}u(X).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-167.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-unit-distance-power-saving",
      "kind": "bound",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $0<C<\\infty$ and $1\\le\\beta<4/3$ such that $u(n)\\le Cn^\\beta$ for every nonnegative integer $n$.",
          "source_id": "PM-167.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-thm-main"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-inc-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $x_1,\\ldots,x_n$ be distinct points of $\\mathbb R^2$, and let $\\mathcal E$ be any collection of pairs $(i,j)$ for which $\\lVert x_i-x_j\\rVert_2=1$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-inc-cutting",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal P$ be a finite planar point set and let $\\mathcal Q$ be a nonempty set of $l$ distinct unit circles. For every $R\\ge1$, sample $s=\\lceil C R\\log(2+l)\\rceil$ circles independently and uniformly with replacement, where $C$ is a sufficiently large absolute constant. With probability at least $9/10$, after deleting the points on sampled circles, all remaining points can be assigned to at most $C'(1+s^2)$ open regions, each of which intersects at most $l/R$ of the original circles. Each assigned point belongs to its assigned region.",
        "source_id": "PM-167.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-inc-piece",
        "exact_quote": "From a sequence of $n=t^3$ real planar points with at least $t^{4-o(1)}$ ordered unit pairs, one can obtain real algebraic point and center sets $P,Q$, and a nonempty graph $E\\subseteq P\\times Q$ consisting of unit incidences, with the following properties. There is a parameter $a=a(t)>0$ such that $a\\to0$, $a\\log t\\to\\infty$, and $$\\label{inc:scales}\n |P|=t^{1+2a+o(a)},\\qquad\n |Q|=t^{2+a+o(a)},\\qquad\n |E|=t^{2+2a+o(a)}.$$ Writing $d(p),d(q)$ for graph degrees, uniformly over all vertices, $$\\label{inc:degrees}\n d(p)=t^{1+o(a)},\\qquad d(q)=t^{a+o(a)}.$$ The uniform law on $E$ has marginals $\\mu(p)=d(p)/|E|$ and $\\kappa(q)=d(q)/|E|$ satisfying $$\\label{inc:marginal-atoms}\n \\mu(p)\\ge\\frac{\\gamma}{|P|},\\qquad\n \\kappa(q)\\ge\\frac{\\gamma}{|Q|},\\qquad\n \\gamma=\\frac{27}{50}=0.54.$$ Set $\\eta=\\gamma-\\tfrac12=1/25$. There is a single sequence $\\xi_t\\to0$ such that, for every $F\\subseteq P$ and $G\\subseteq Q$, with counting fractions $u=|F|/|P|$ and $v=|G|/|Q|$, $$\\label{inc:expansion}\n \\mathbb P_E(p\\in F,q\\in G)\n \\le \\sqrt{uv}\\bigl((uv)^\\eta+\\xi_t\\bigr).$$ The sequence $\\xi_t$ is independent of the chosen subsets.",
        "source_id": "PM-167.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-inc-piece",
          "exact_quote": "From a sequence of $n=t^3$ real planar points with at least $t^{4-o(1)}$ ordered unit pairs, one can obtain real algebraic point and center sets $P,Q$, and a nonempty graph $E\\subseteq P\\times Q$ consisting of unit incidences, with the following properties. There is a parameter $a=a(t)>0$ such that $a\\to0$, $a\\log t\\to\\infty$, and $$\\label{inc:scales}\n |P|=t^{1+2a+o(a)},\\qquad\n |Q|=t^{2+a+o(a)},\\qquad\n |E|=t^{2+2a+o(a)}.$$ Writing $d(p),d(q)$ for graph degrees, uniformly over all vertices, $$\\label{inc:degrees}\n d(p)=t^{1+o(a)},\\qquad d(q)=t^{a+o(a)}.$$ The uniform law on $E$ has marginals $\\mu(p)=d(p)/|E|$ and $\\kappa(q)=d(q)/|E|$ satisfying $$\\label{inc:marginal-atoms}\n \\mu(p)\\ge\\frac{\\gamma}{|P|},\\qquad\n \\kappa(q)\\ge\\frac{\\gamma}{|Q|},\\qquad\n \\gamma=\\frac{27}{50}=0.54.$$ Set $\\eta=\\gamma-\\tfrac12=1/25$. There is a single sequence $\\xi_t\\to0$ such that, for every $F\\subseteq P$ and $G\\subseteq Q$, with counting fractions $u=|F|/|P|$ and $v=|G|/|Q|$, $$\\label{inc:expansion}\n \\mathbb P_E(p\\in F,q\\in G)\n \\le \\sqrt{uv}\\bigl((uv)^\\eta+\\xi_t\\bigr).$$ The sequence $\\xi_t$ is independent of the chosen subsets.",
          "source_id": "PM-167.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "From a sequence of $n=t^3$ real planar points with at least $t^{4-o(1)}$ ordered unit pairs, one can obtain real algebraic point and center sets $P,Q$, and a nonempty graph $E\\subseteq P\\times Q$ consisting of unit incidences, with the following properties. There is a parameter $a=a(t)>0$ such that $a\\to0$, $a\\log t\\to\\infty$, and $$\\label{inc:scales}\n |P|=t^{1+2a+o(a)},\\qquad\n |Q|=t^{2+a+o(a)},\\qquad\n |E|=t^{2+2a+o(a)}.$$ Writing $d(p),d(q)$ for graph degrees, uniformly over all vertices, $$\\label{inc:degrees}\n d(p)=t^{1+o(a)},\\qquad d(q)=t^{a+o(a)}.$$ The uniform law on $E$ has marginals $\\mu(p)=d(p)/|E|$ and $\\kappa(q)=d(q)/|E|$ satisfying $$\\label{inc:marginal-atoms}\n \\mu(p)\\ge\\frac{\\gamma}{|P|},\\qquad\n \\kappa(q)\\ge\\frac{\\gamma}{|Q|},\\qquad\n \\gamma=\\frac{27}{50}=0.54.$$ Set $\\eta=\\gamma-\\tfrac12=1/25$. There is a single sequence $\\xi_t\\to0$ such that, for every $F\\subseteq P$ and $G\\subseteq Q$, with counting fractions $u=|F|/|P|$ and $v=|G|/|Q|$, $$\\label{inc:expansion}\n \\mathbb P_E(p\\in F,q\\in G)\n \\le \\sqrt{uv}\\bigl((uv)^\\eta+\\xi_t\\bigr).$$ The sequence $\\xi_t$ is independent of the chosen subsets.",
      "explanation": "Specialization, cutting, degree trimming and rectangle maximization produce an unbalanced incidence graph with controlled degrees, lower marginal atoms and uniform subset expansion.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-inc-matching",
        "exact_quote": "For the sequence of edge laws in [inc:piece](#inc:piece), there are absolute constants $s_0>0,C<\\infty$ and an index after which the following holds uniformly for every pair of label maps $\\ell_P:P\\to\\mathcal A$, $\\ell_Q:Q\\to\\mathcal A$ into a finite set. If $$s=\\mathbb P_E\\bigl(\\ell_P(p)\\ne\\ell_Q(q)\\bigr)\\le s_0,$$ some label $a_0\\in\\mathcal A$ satisfies $$\\mu\\{\\ell_P\\ne a_0\\}+\\kappa\\{\\ell_Q\\ne a_0\\}\\le Cs.$$ Moreover, for independent $p,p'\\sim\\mu$, at every value of $s$, $$\\label{inc:point-label-mismatch}\n \\mathbb P\\bigl(\\ell_P(p)\\ne\\ell_P(p')\\bigr)\\le Cs.$$ The analogous statement holds for independent centers of law $\\kappa$.",
        "source_id": "PM-167.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-inc-matching",
          "exact_quote": "For the sequence of edge laws in [inc:piece](#inc:piece), there are absolute constants $s_0>0,C<\\infty$ and an index after which the following holds uniformly for every pair of label maps $\\ell_P:P\\to\\mathcal A$, $\\ell_Q:Q\\to\\mathcal A$ into a finite set. If $$s=\\mathbb P_E\\bigl(\\ell_P(p)\\ne\\ell_Q(q)\\bigr)\\le s_0,$$ some label $a_0\\in\\mathcal A$ satisfies $$\\mu\\{\\ell_P\\ne a_0\\}+\\kappa\\{\\ell_Q\\ne a_0\\}\\le Cs.$$ Moreover, for independent $p,p'\\sim\\mu$, at every value of $s$, $$\\label{inc:point-label-mismatch}\n \\mathbb P\\bigl(\\ell_P(p)\\ne\\ell_P(p')\\bigr)\\le Cs.$$ The analogous statement holds for independent centers of law $\\kappa$.",
          "source_id": "PM-167.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For the sequence of edge laws in [inc:piece](#inc:piece), there are absolute constants $s_0>0,C<\\infty$ and an index after which the following holds uniformly for every pair of label maps $\\ell_P:P\\to\\mathcal A$, $\\ell_Q:Q\\to\\mathcal A$ into a finite set. If $$s=\\mathbb P_E\\bigl(\\ell_P(p)\\ne\\ell_Q(q)\\bigr)\\le s_0,$$ some label $a_0\\in\\mathcal A$ satisfies $$\\mu\\{\\ell_P\\ne a_0\\}+\\kappa\\{\\ell_Q\\ne a_0\\}\\le Cs.$$ Moreover, for independent $p,p'\\sim\\mu$, at every value of $s$, $$\\label{inc:point-label-mismatch}\n \\mathbb P\\bigl(\\ell_P(p)\\ne\\ell_P(p')\\bigr)\\le Cs.$$ The analogous statement holds for independent centers of law $\\kappa$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-inc-rare",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\delta_t\\to0$ be any nonnegative sequence. The estimates below hold uniformly over all indicated subsets, with error factors depending only on $\\delta_t,\\xi_t$ and the fixed constants in <a href=\"#inc:marginal-expansion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"inc:marginal-expansion\">[inc:marginal-expansion]</a>:*\n\n1.  *If $F\\subseteq P$, $G\\subseteq Q$, and $h=\\mu(F)\\le\\delta_t$, while $k=\\kappa(G)$ is arbitrary, then $$\\label{inc:one-small-set}\n     \\mathbb P_E(F,G)\\le C(\\delta_t^\\eta+\\xi_t)\\sqrt{hk}\n                     =o(1)\\sqrt{hk}.$$ The same assertion holds with the sides interchanged.*\n\n2.  *If also $k\\le\\delta_t$, then $$\\label{inc:joint-rare-edge}\n     \\mathbb P_E(F,G)\\le\\tfrac C2(\\delta_t^{2\\eta}+\\xi_t)(h+k)\n                     =o(1)(h+k).$$*\n\n3.  *If $F\\subseteq P$ and $h=\\mu(F)\\le\\delta_t$, then under the wedge law, $$\\label{inc:joint-rare-wedge}\n     \\mathbb P(p\\in F,r\\in F)=o(1)h.$$*\n\n*In particular, the error factors may be taken outside sums or integrals over any family of sets whose marginal masses obey the same uniform bound $\\delta_t$.",
        "source_id": "PM-167.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-inc-rare",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\delta_t\\to0$ be any nonnegative sequence. The estimates below hold uniformly over all indicated subsets, with error factors depending only on $\\delta_t,\\xi_t$ and the fixed constants in <a href=\"#inc:marginal-expansion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"inc:marginal-expansion\">[inc:marginal-expansion]</a>:*\n\n1.  *If $F\\subseteq P$, $G\\subseteq Q$, and $h=\\mu(F)\\le\\delta_t$, while $k=\\kappa(G)$ is arbitrary, then $$\\label{inc:one-small-set}\n     \\mathbb P_E(F,G)\\le C(\\delta_t^\\eta+\\xi_t)\\sqrt{hk}\n                     =o(1)\\sqrt{hk}.$$ The same assertion holds with the sides interchanged.*\n\n2.  *If also $k\\le\\delta_t$, then $$\\label{inc:joint-rare-edge}\n     \\mathbb P_E(F,G)\\le\\tfrac C2(\\delta_t^{2\\eta}+\\xi_t)(h+k)\n                     =o(1)(h+k).$$*\n\n3.  *If $F\\subseteq P$ and $h=\\mu(F)\\le\\delta_t$, then under the wedge law, $$\\label{inc:joint-rare-wedge}\n     \\mathbb P(p\\in F,r\\in F)=o(1)h.$$*\n\n*In particular, the error factors may be taken outside sums or integrals over any family of sets whose marginal masses obey the same uniform bound $\\delta_t$.",
          "source_id": "PM-167.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\delta_t\\to0$ be any nonnegative sequence. The estimates below hold uniformly over all indicated subsets, with error factors depending only on $\\delta_t,\\xi_t$ and the fixed constants in <a href=\"#inc:marginal-expansion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"inc:marginal-expansion\">[inc:marginal-expansion]</a>:*\n\n1.  *If $F\\subseteq P$, $G\\subseteq Q$, and $h=\\mu(F)\\le\\delta_t$, while $k=\\kappa(G)$ is arbitrary, then $$\\label{inc:one-small-set}\n     \\mathbb P_E(F,G)\\le C(\\delta_t^\\eta+\\xi_t)\\sqrt{hk}\n                     =o(1)\\sqrt{hk}.$$ The same assertion holds with the sides interchanged.*\n\n2.  *If also $k\\le\\delta_t$, then $$\\label{inc:joint-rare-edge}\n     \\mathbb P_E(F,G)\\le\\tfrac C2(\\delta_t^{2\\eta}+\\xi_t)(h+k)\n                     =o(1)(h+k).$$*\n\n3.  *If $F\\subseteq P$ and $h=\\mu(F)\\le\\delta_t$, then under the wedge law, $$\\label{inc:joint-rare-wedge}\n     \\mathbb P(p\\in F,r\\in F)=o(1)h.$$*\n\n*In particular, the error factors may be taken outside sums or integrals over any family of sets whose marginal masses obey the same uniform bound $\\delta_t$.",
      "explanation": "Expansion suppresses joint rare events along edges and wedges uniformly over the exceptional subsets. Uniformity permits the error factors to pass outside level integrals.",

      "id": "claim-uniformly-rare-subsets",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-inc-entropy",
        "exact_quote": "For the wedge law <a href=\"#inc:wedge-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"inc:wedge-law\">[inc:wedge-law]</a>, let $d_{\\min,Q}=\\min_{q\\in Q}d(q)$. If $p\\ne r$, then $$\\label{inc:wedge-atoms}\n \\mathbb P(p,r)\\le\\frac{2}{|E|d_{\\min,Q}}\n                 =t^{-2-3a+o(a)}.$$ The diagonal probability is $$\\label{inc:wedge-diagonal}\n \\mathbb P(p=r)=\\frac{|Q|}{|E|}=t^{-a+o(a)}=o(1).$$ The mutual information of the point endpoints satisfies $$\\label{inc:information-bound}\n D:=\\mathop{\\mathrm{D_{\\mathrm{KL}}}}\\bigl(\\mathcal L(p,r)\\,\\Vert\\,\\mu\\otimes\\mu\\bigr)\n   =o(\\log t).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-167.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-inc-entropy",
          "exact_quote": "For the wedge law <a href=\"#inc:wedge-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"inc:wedge-law\">[inc:wedge-law]</a>, let $d_{\\min,Q}=\\min_{q\\in Q}d(q)$. If $p\\ne r$, then $$\\label{inc:wedge-atoms}\n \\mathbb P(p,r)\\le\\frac{2}{|E|d_{\\min,Q}}\n                 =t^{-2-3a+o(a)}.$$ The diagonal probability is $$\\label{inc:wedge-diagonal}\n \\mathbb P(p=r)=\\frac{|Q|}{|E|}=t^{-a+o(a)}=o(1).$$ The mutual information of the point endpoints satisfies $$\\label{inc:information-bound}\n D:=\\mathop{\\mathrm{D_{\\mathrm{KL}}}}\\bigl(\\mathcal L(p,r)\\,\\Vert\\,\\mu\\otimes\\mu\\bigr)\n   =o(\\log t).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-167.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For the wedge law <a href=\"#inc:wedge-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"inc:wedge-law\">[inc:wedge-law]</a>, let $d_{\\min,Q}=\\min_{q\\in Q}d(q)$. If $p\\ne r$, then $$\\label{inc:wedge-atoms}\n \\mathbb P(p,r)\\le\\frac{2}{|E|d_{\\min,Q}}\n                 =t^{-2-3a+o(a)}.$$ The diagonal probability is $$\\label{inc:wedge-diagonal}\n \\mathbb P(p=r)=\\frac{|Q|}{|E|}=t^{-a+o(a)}=o(1).$$ The mutual information of the point endpoints satisfies $$\\label{inc:information-bound}\n D:=\\mathop{\\mathrm{D_{\\mathrm{KL}}}}\\bigl(\\mathcal L(p,r)\\,\\Vert\\,\\mu\\otimes\\mu\\bigr)\n   =o(\\log t).$$",
      "explanation": "The geometric codegree bound controls off-diagonal wedge atoms. Together with degree estimates and a vanishing diagonal mass, this leaves little mutual information between point endpoints.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $\\omega,\\pi,D$ be as in <a href=\"#pred:wedge-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"pred:wedge-law\">[pred:wedge-law]</a>–<a href=\"#pred:information\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"pred:information\">[pred:information]</a>, let $K,M\\ge1$ be integers, and let $T_1,\\ldots,T_K$ be independent tests with the same law as $T$. Each test specifies, in addition to $c_T$, a feature map $f_T:[M]\\to\\mathbb R^d$ and a coefficient map $g_T:Q\\to\\mathbb R^d$, for an integer $d\\ge1$ and constants $B,B'\\ge0$, satisfying $$\\lVert f_T(c)\\rVert\\le B,\\qquad \\lVert g_T(q)\\rVert\\le B'$$ for all codes, centers and tests. Choose $J$ uniformly from $[K]$, independently of all tests, and retain only the strict prefix $$H(p)=(c_{T_1}(p),\\ldots,c_{T_{J-1}}(p)).$$ Conditioned on $J,T_1,\\ldots,T_{J-1}$, let $T$ be a fresh test and define $$m_{T,h}=\\sum_{x\\in P}\\mu_h(x)f_T(c_T(x)).$$ Then $$\\label{pred:device-bound}\n \\mathbb E_{J,T_1,\\ldots,T_{J-1},T}\n \\left|\\mathbb E_{(p,q)\\sim\\omega}\n \\langle f_T(c_T(p))-m_{T,H(p)},g_T(q)\\rangle\\right|\n \\le4BB'\\left(\\frac DK\\right)^{1/4}.$$ Every realized history has at most $M^{K-1}$ values. In particular, a history can be fixed before drawing the fresh test so that its average absolute discrepancy in <a href=\"#pred:device-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"pred:device-bound\">[pred:device-bound]</a> is at most the right side.",
        "source_id": "PM-167.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "In the setting of [pred:device](#pred:device), suppose each test specifies a set $\\mathcal A_T(q)\\subseteq[M]$ of accepted codes for every $q\\in Q$.         "exact_quote": "Let $\\omega,\\pi,D$ be as above, let $K\\ge1,L\\ge2$ be integers, and let $T$ be an independent random test giving the state maps just described. Set $$s_0=\\mathbb E_T\\mathbb P_{(p,q)\\sim\\omega}\n                       \\{y_T(p)\\notin R_{y_T(q)}\\}.$$ There exists a fixed history $H:P\\to\\mathcal H$, with $\\lvert H(P)\\rvert\\le L^{2K}$, for which the laws of the *true*, unhashed states $$\\nu_{h,T}=(y_T)_*\\mu_h$$ satisfy $$\\label{pred:cross-bound}\n \\mathbb E_T\\mathbb E_{(p,q)\\sim\\omega}\n       [1-\\nu_{H(p),T}(R_{y_T(q)})]\n \\le s_0+\\frac4L+18(D/K)^{1/4}.$$ The history is formed from earlier tests and does not use $T$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ht-product",
        "exact_quote": "There is a countable collection of absolute-value entries $v$ on $\\mathcal N$, each of weight $1/d_{\\mathcal N}$, with the following properties. Its archimedean entries are $|u|_v=|\\sigma(u)|$ for the $d_{\\mathcal N}$ embeddings $\\sigma:\\mathcal N\\to\\mathbb C$, so their total weight is one. All other entries are nonarchimedean. For each nonzero $u\\in\\mathcal N$, only finitely many nonarchimedean entries have $|u|_v\\ne1$, and $$\\label{ht:product-formula}\n \\sum_v^*\\log|u|_v=0.$$ Here and below a star denotes the sum with the stated weights.",
        "source_id": "PM-167.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ht-product",
          "exact_quote": "There is a countable collection of absolute-value entries $v$ on $\\mathcal N$, each of weight $1/d_{\\mathcal N}$, with the following properties. For each nonzero $u\\in\\mathcal N$, only finitely many nonarchimedean entries have $|u|_v\\ne1$, and $$\\label{ht:product-formula}\n \\sum_v^*\\log|u|_v=0.$$ Here and below a star denotes the sum with the stated weights.",
          "source_id": "PM-167.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a countable collection of absolute-value entries $v$ on $\\mathcal N$, each of weight $1/d_{\\mathcal N}$, with the following properties.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ht-height",
        "exact_quote": "The value $h(u)$ is independent of the chosen finite normal field and of the extensions of the prime absolute values in [ht:product](#ht:product). For $u\\in\\mathcal N^\\times$, $$\\label{ht:height-inversion}\n h(u^{-1})=h(u),\\qquad\n \\sum_v^*|\\log|u|_v|=2h(u).$$ For arbitrary $u,v\\in\\mathcal N$, $$\\label{ht:height-inequalities}\n h(uv)\\le h(u)+h(v),\\qquad\n h(u+v)\\le h(u)+h(v)+\\log2.$$ If a random $X\\in\\mathcal N$ is independent of a uniformly sampled embedding $\\sigma:\\mathcal N\\to\\mathbb C$, then, for $B>1$ and $0<r<1$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb P\\{|\\sigma(X)|>B\\}&\\le\\frac{\\mathbb Eh(X)}{\\log B},\n       \\label{ht:large-tail}\\\\\n \\mathbb P\\{X\\ne0,\\ |\\sigma(X)|<r\\}\n      &\\le\\frac{\\mathbb Eh(X)}{\\log(1/r)}.\n       \\label{ht:small-tail}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-167.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ht-height",
          "exact_quote": "The value $h(u)$ is independent of the chosen finite normal field and of the extensions of the prime absolute values in [ht:product](#ht:product).           "source_id": "PM-167.2"

      "exact_statement": "The value $h(u)$ is independent of the chosen finite normal field and of the extensions of the prime absolute values in [ht:product](#ht:product).       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ht-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a probability law $\\omega$ on two finite vertex sets $P\\times Q$, with point marginal $\\mu$, has the following property: for all assignments of finite labels to its vertices, two independent $\\mu$-points disagree with probability at most $C_0$ times the edge disagreement probability. For any coordinate $U:P\\cup Q\\to\\mathcal N$, $$\\mathbb E_{p,p'\\sim\\mu}h(U_p-U_{p'})\n   \\le C_1\\bigl(\\mathbb E_{(p,q)\\sim\\omega}h(U_p-U_q)+1\\bigr),$$ where $C_1$ depends only on $C_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-167.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ht-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose a probability law $\\omega$ on two finite vertex sets $P\\times Q$, with point marginal $\\mu$, has the following property: for all assignments of finite labels to its vertices, two independent $\\mu$-points disagree with probability at most $C_0$ times the edge disagreement probability. For any coordinate $U:P\\cup Q\\to\\mathcal N$, $$\\mathbb E_{p,p'\\sim\\mu}h(U_p-U_{p'})\n   \\le C_1\\bigl(\\mathbb E_{(p,q)\\sim\\omega}h(U_p-U_q)+1\\bigr),$$ where $C_1$ depends only on $C_0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-167.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Suppose a probability law $\\omega$ on two finite vertex sets $P\\times Q$, with point marginal $\\mu$, has the following property: for all assignments of finite labels to its vertices, two independent $\\mu$-points disagree with probability at most $C_0$ times the edge disagreement probability.       "explanation": "Coordinate partitions turn height tails into label disagreements. The assumed label comparison therefore transfers mean edge-difference height to mean point-difference height.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ext-circles",
        "exact_quote": "Call a real unit circle *popular* if it contains at least $t^{9/10}$ points of $P$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ext-pairs",
        "exact_quote": "For the incidence graphs and levels constructed above, there are $J\\to\\infty$, a sequence $\\omega_t\\to0$, and a simple bipartite graph $\\mathcal E$ between two copies of $P$, each of size at most $t^{1+o(1)}$, with at least $t^{2-o(1)}$ edges, having the following properties. Each edge $(p,r)$ has distinct endpoints and an assigned center $q=q(p,r)\\in Q$ with $pq,rq\\in E$. Set $$\\lambda_{pr}=\\frac{z_p-z_{q(p,r)}}{z_r-z_{q(p,r)}}.$$ No edge has both endpoints on any real unit circle containing at least $t^{9/10}$ points of $P$. Uniformly over the edges and complete rectangles of $\\mathcal E$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n (z_p-z_r)(w_p-w_r)&=2-\\lambda_{pr}-\\lambda_{pr}^{-1},\n                                               \\label{ext:pair-identity}\\\\\n h(\\lambda_{pr})&\\ge J/3,                      \\label{ext:pair-height}\\\\\n h\\!\\left(\\frac{\\lambda_{pr}\\lambda_{p'r'}}\n                    {\\lambda_{pr'}\\lambda_{p'r}}\\right)\n &\\le\\omega_tJ.                               \\label{ext:rectangle-height}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In the last line all four indicated edges are required to be present; their assigned centers need not agree. The edge ratios $\\lambda_{pr}$ are unrelated to the fixed score constant $\\lambda$.",
        "source_id": "PM-167.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ext-pairs",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Planar unit-distance counting functions** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a finite set $X\subset\mathbb R^2$, write $$u(X)=\#\bigl\{\{x,y\}\subset X:\ \lVert x-y\rVert_2=1\bigr\}, \qquad u(n)=\max_{\substack{X\subset\mathbb R^2\\ \|X\|=n}}u(X).$$
- **Uniform-edge wedge law** ([source](#inc:wedge-law)): Define the *wedge law* on triples $(p,q,r)\in P\times Q\times P$ by first choosing $q\sim\kappa$ and then independently choosing $p,r$ according to the conditional edge law given $q$. Explicitly, $$\label{inc:wedge-law} \mathbb P(p,q,r)= \begin{cases} (\|E\|d(q))^{-1},&(p,q),(r,q)\in E,\\ 0,&\text{otherwise}. \end{cases}$$ Each point marginal is $\mu$, and each of the two point–center marginals is the uniform edge law.
- **Relative entropy** ([source](#inc:relative-entropy)): For probability laws $a,b$ on the same finite set, define their relative entropy by $$\label{inc:relative-entropy} \mathop{\mathrm{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}}(a\Vert b)=\sum_x a(x)\log\frac{a(x)}{b(x)},$$ with a zero summand when $a(x)=0$ and value $+\infty$ if $a(x)>0=b(x)$.
- **General point–center wedge and information** ([source](#pred:wedge-law)): The associated wedge law and its point-pair marginal are $$\label{pred:wedge-law} \Omega(p,q,r)=\kappa(q)\omega(p\mid q)\omega(r\mid q), \qquad \pi(p,r)=\sum_{q\in Q}\Omega(p,q,r).$$
- **History posterior** ([source](#pred:posterior-point)): For a fixed history map $H:P\to\mathcal H$, put $$\label{pred:posterior-point} \mu_h(x)=\frac{\mu(x)\mathbf1_{\{H(x)=h\}}}{\mu(H=h)} \quad\text{when }\mu(H=h)>0.$$
- **Shared prediction coupling** ([source](#pred:shared-prediction-law)): For any fixed history $H$ and integer $r\ge1$, draw one point, one prediction, and $r$ neighbors according to $$\label{pred:shared-prediction-law} \mathbb P\{p,\widehat p,q_1,\ldots,q_r\} =\mu(p)\mu_{H(p)}(\widehat p) \prod_{j=1}^r\omega(q_j\mid p),$$ and draw the fresh test independently of this entire tuple.
- **Strict cross** ([source](#pm-h-20)): For a state $y=(a,b)$, define its *strict cross* $$\label{pred:strict-cross} R_y=\{(a',b'): (a'=a\text{ and }b'\ne b) \text{ or }(a'\ne a\text{ and }b'=b)\}.$$
- **Reciprocal unit-edge jumps** ([source](#sec:heights)): For every edge $e=(p,q)$, put $$\label{ht:jumps} d_e=z_p-z_q,\qquad w_p-w_q=d_e^{-1}.$$
- **Threshold bits and minority scale** ([source](#ht:levels)): At a level $(v,s)$ put $$\label{ht:levels} \begin{aligned} Y_e(v,s)&=\mathbf1_{\{n_e(v)>s\}},& \pi(v,s)&=\mathbb P_E\{Y_e(v,s)=1\},\\ f(v,s)&=\min(\pi(v,s),1-\pi(v,s)),& S&=\int f. \end{aligned}$$
- **Coordinate states** ([source](#pm-h-26)): At a level $(v,s)$ give each vertex $x$ a state $$y_x(v,s)=(A_x(v,s),B_{1,x}(v,s)).$$
- **Common, damaged and good rare levels** ([source](#bud:widths)): Define the common, damaged, and good rare levels, respectively, by $$\mathcal C=\{f\ge\epsilon\},\qquad \mathcal B=\{f<\epsilon,\ m>1/k\},\qquad \mathcal G=\{f<\epsilon,\ m\le1/k\}.$$
- **Neighbor-history template** ([source](#bud:template-count)): For each center $q$, a *template* is an ordered list $\theta=(h_1,\ldots,h_k)$ obtained by sampling $k$ independent neighbors of $q$ and recording their histories.
- **Candidate center states** ([source](#com:candidates)): For its template $\theta=(h_1,\ldots,h_k)$, define the finite set of candidate center states $$\label{com:candidates} \mathcal Y_\theta =\{y\text{ a center state}:\nu_{h_j}(R_y)\ge 1-k^{-2} \text{ for every }1\le j\le k\}.$$
- **Two-prediction score** ([source](#com:T)): Define $$\label{com:T} T=\int_{\mathcal C}\mathbb E_\mu\left[ \lVert (\nu_{H(p)})_A\rVert_2^2+ \lVert (\nu_{H(p)})_{B_1}\rVert_2^2-1\right].$$
- **Unary point profile** ([source](#pm-h-36)): Define a real-valued profile, indexed by places, by $$\label{ext:profile-definition} a_p(v)=\int_{\substack{s:\,(v,s)\text{ is}\\\text{good rare}}} \sigma(v,s)X_p(v,s)\,\mathrm ds.$$
- **Negligible-height field** ([source](#pm-h-39)): Define $$k_0=\bigl\{[u_n]\in F:h(u_n)=o(J_n)\bigr\}.$$
- **Integer-valued valuation** ([source](#pm-h-42)): An integer-valued valuation of $M$ is a map $v:M^\times\to\mathbb Z$ with $v(ab)=v(a)+v(b)$ and $v(a+b)\ge\min\{v(a),v(b)\}$, where $v(0)=+\infty$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-unit-distance-exponent | \beta | < | $4/3$ | Absolute exponent and absolute multiplicative constant. |
| q-cutting-sample-size | s | = | $\lceil C R\log(2+l)\rceil$ | The sampling parameter is at least one; the absolute constant must be sufficiently large. |
| q-cutting-success-probability | Cutting success probability | ≥ | $9/10$ | The probability includes deletion and assignment to open conflict-bounded regions. |
| q-incidence-count | I | ≤ | $C\log^2(2+m+l) \bigl(m^{2/3}l^{2/3}+m+l\bigr)$ | An absolute multiplicative constant; includes empty-set cases. |
| q-marginal-atom-factor | \gamma | = | $\frac{27}{50}$ | Fixed lower-marginal factor. |
| q-expansion-surplus | \eta | = | $1/25$ | Fixed surplus over the square-root exponent. |
| q-wedge-collision-probability | \mathbb P(p=r) | = | $\frac{\|Q\|}{\|E\|}$ | Exact identity with asymptotic decay under the incidence-piece degree scales. |
| q-wedge-mutual-information | D | = | $o(\log t)$ | Along the chosen incidence sequence; no convergence rate is supplied. |
| q-prediction-discrepancy | Average absolute prediction discrepancy | ≤ | $4BB'\left(\frac DK\right)^{1/4}$ | Bounded feature and coefficient norms; an independent fresh test after a strict prefix. |
| q-acceptance-failure | Predicted acceptance failure | ≤ | $s_0+4\sqrt M(D/K)^{1/4}$ | The selected history precedes the fresh independent test. |
| q-strict-cross-failure | True-state strict-cross prediction failure | ≤ | $s_0+\frac4L+18(D/K)^{1/4}$ | The history depends on earlier tests and not on the current true test. |
| q-integrated-inconsistency | \int m | = | $O(1)$ | An absolute bound independent of the configuration scale. |
| q-strict-cross-sampling-error | Probability of a sampled low-mass strict cross | ≤ | $2(2k+1)^2\,2^{-k\alpha/2}$ | Uniform over possible strict crosses. |
| q-restricted-support-transfer-error | Actual-state support failure probability | ≤ | $8\eta$ | Support selection may depend on both center and template. |
| q-extracted-ratio-height | h(\lambda_{pr}) | ≥ | $J/3$ | Uniform over retained edges along a diverging height scale. |
| q-rectangle-defect-height | Height of an alternating rectangle product | ≤ | $\omega_tJ$ | The error sequence tends to zero uniformly over rectangles. |
| q-left-grid-size | N | = | $20$ | Fixed left-side grid size. |
| q-right-grid-size | j | = | $5$ | Fixed right-side grid size. |
| q-surviving-transcendence-budget | \operatorname{trdeg}_{K_0}K_0(p_1,\ldots,p_N) | ≥ | $Nd_1-3j$ | The fixed generic grid; the derivative data may depend on the left positions. |
| q-multiquadratic-extension-degree | [M(R_1,\ldots,R_j):M] | = | $2^j$ | The same enlarged field in which private valuations were constructed. |

        }

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-specialize** [claim](#stmt-inc-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inc-specialization): Real closed field transfer for rational equations, strict distinctness inequalities and neighborhood rectangles. -> Distinct nearby real algebraic points preserving selected unit pairs.
- **step-cutting** [claim](#stmt-inc-cutting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inc-cutting): Circle sampling, open gap decomposition and a union bound over bounded-description candidate regions. -> A conflict-bounded open-region assignment after sampled-circle deletion.
- **step-incidence-bound** [claim](#stmt-inc-incidence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inc-incidence): Apply the point–curve incidence theorem using geometric pair bounds, cutting circles into arcs if required. -> The logarithmically weakened incidence estimate.
- **step-incidence-piece** [claim](#stmt-inc-piece) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inc-piece): Cutting, degree trimming and finite rectangle-density maximization. -> Controlled degrees, lower marginal atoms and uniform expansion.
- **step-matching-labels** [claim](#stmt-inc-matching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inc-matching): Sum expansion over matching classes, find a dominant class, then bound simultaneous complementary exceptions. -> Mismatch-proportional marginal concentration.
- **step-rare-subsets** [claim](#stmt-inc-rare) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inc-rare): Apply marginal expansion and split a conditional rare-event probability at a slowly vanishing threshold. -> Uniform joint edge and wedge rarity bounds.
- **step-wedge-information** [claim](#stmt-inc-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-inc-entropy): Geometric codegree bounds and entropy comparison between joint and marginal laws. -> Small endpoint mutual information and wedge atom bounds.
- **step-prediction-device** [claim](#stmt-pred-device) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pred-device): Transcript data processing, entropy chain rule, total variation control and a center-conditioned residual-square identity. -> A strict-prefix prediction discrepancy bound with a fresh independent test.
- **step-acceptance** [claim](#stmt-pred-acceptance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pred-acceptance): Use code basis vectors and center acceptance indicators as features. -> A fixed history with controlled predicted acceptance failure.
- **step-strict-cross-prediction** [claim](#stmt-pred-cross) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pred-cross): Independent coordinate hashing and comparison of true and hashed strict-cross indicators. -> An unhashed strict-cross prediction bound independent of label-set cardinality.
- **step-product-formula** [claim](#stmt-ht-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ht-product): Normalize finite places by local degrees and count complex embeddings separately. -> An equal-weight list of absolute values satisfying the product formula.
- **step-height-bounds** [claim](#stmt-ht-height) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ht-height): Field-extension weight comparison, product formula, triangle inequalities and Markov bounds. -> Field-independent heights, algebraic height inequalities and embedding tails.
- **step-height-transfer** [claim](#stmt-ht-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ht-transfer): Integrate coordinate-label mismatch using nonarchimedean equivalence classes and randomly shifted complex grids. -> Mean point-difference height control.
- **step-exclude-bounded-scale** [claim](#stmt-ht-bounded) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ht-bounded): Normalize heights, predict at a fresh embedding and contradict separated neighbor equations by a determinant estimate. -> The scale has no bounded subsequence.
- **step-coordinate-states** [claim](#stmt-ht-states) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ht-states): Average shifted grid inconsistency and fix shifts with bounded integrated cost. -> Measurable coordinate states with bounded integrated inconsistency.
- **step-parameter-hierarchy** [claim](#stmt-bud-parameters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bud-parameters): Choose history and hashing parameters first, then let the sample count diverge sufficiently slowly. -> Compatible vanishing-error and small-template requirements.
- **step-rare-moments** [claim](#stmt-bud-rare-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bud-rare-moments): Find dominant labels and apply uniform joint-rarity bounds under the original edge and wedge laws. -> Controlled exception first moments and negligible joint exceptions.
- **step-star-laws** [claim](#stmt-bud-pair-laws) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bud-pair-laws): Sum the edge–template joint law and bound conditional collision atoms pointwise in the point base. -> Preserved edge-slot marginals and template collision bounds.
- **step-interval-envelope** [claim](#stmt-bud-intervals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bud-intervals): Bound flanking-interval overlaps and exhaust the central open interval using survival-function differences. -> An interior-only excess bound and central-window mass bound.
- **step-upper-budget** [claim](#stmt-bud-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bud-upper): Localize positive scores to flanking intervals, cancel distinct-pair logarithms and bound diagonal pairs separately. -> Upper score bounds for every star orientation.
- **step-lower-rare-budget** [claim](#stmt-bud-rare-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bud-rare-lower): Charge exceptional opposite endpoints and inconsistent slots in a pointwise rare-level score inequality. -> Orientation-sensitive lower bounds with cancelling marginal terms.
- **step-ghost-sampling** [claim](#stmt-com-ghost) / [proof](#proof-stmt-com-ghost): Double sampling, indexed strict-cross trace counting and synthetic posterior samples. -> Uniform high-mass prediction-mixture support for candidate crosses.
- **step-support-transfer** [claim](#stmt-com-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-com-transfer): Use history-defined exceptional sets for concentrated rows, columns and small state supports. -> A bound on actual point states outside the selected support.
- **step-common-variation** [claim](#stmt-com-variation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-com-variation): Classify strict-cross intersections and average score comparisons with actual template slice weights. -> Negligible common variation and center-to-point rare-mass comparison.
- **step-two-prediction-score** [claim](#stmt-com-prediction-score) / [proof](#proof-stmt-com-prediction-score): Apply the product formula to distinct history-conditioned point pairs and bound diagonal mass. -> A negligible upper bound on the two-prediction score.
- **step-positive-rare-mass** [claim](#stmt-com-rare-positive) / [proof](#proof-stmt-com-rare-positive): Contradict vanishing rare mass using a second strict-cross score and expansion-driven majority-bit concentration. -> A positive asymptotic proportion of rare point-exception mass.
- **step-pointwise-rare-bound** [claim](#stmt-ext-pointwise) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-pointwise): Compare pointwise rare indicator charges with a distinct-neighbor product-formula budget. -> A deterministic rare-point exception bound.
- **step-tail-control** [claim](#stmt-ext-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-tail): Cap neighbor exception heights, average distinctness pointwise and use layer-cake expansion. -> Negligible tail first moment at the selected truncation scale.
- **step-wedge-profiles** [claim](#stmt-ext-profiles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-profiles): Subtract rare-level edge identities so the center contribution cancels and bound integrated residuals. -> Wedge profile approximation and lower size estimates.
- **step-accurate-band** [claim](#stmt-ext-band) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-band): Remove relative-error events in weighted measure, truncate tails and select a dyadic height band. -> A uniformly accurate band of distinct-endpoint wedges.
- **step-delete-popular-circles** [claim](#stmt-ext-circles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-circles): Count popular circles and multiple-circle points, then use original conditional neighbor probabilities. -> Popular-circle deletion with negligible relative band loss.
- **step-organized-pairs** [claim](#stmt-ext-pairs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-pairs): Convert wedge probability to distinct pair counts, choose witnesses and cancel unary profiles around rectangles. -> A dense pair graph with large ratio heights and negligible rectangle defects.
- **step-negligible-height-field** [claim](#stmt-alg-small-height-field) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-small-height-field): Ultrafilter sequence fields, height inequalities and a fixed-degree monic root-height estimate. -> A relatively algebraically closed negligible-height subfield.
- **step-generic-grid** [claim](#stmt-alg-generic-grid) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-generic-grid): Minimal-dimensional locus restriction, uniform polynomial avoidance and increasing finite-prefix diagonal selection. -> A complete limiting grid with generic selected coordinates.
- **step-field-derivation** [claim](#stmt-alg-derivation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-derivation): Prescribe derivatives on transcendence bases, extend over algebraic fields and avoid finitely many linear kernels. -> A derivation nonzero on every grid ratio.
- **step-surviving-point** [claim](#stmt-alg-surviving-point) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-surviving-point): Transcendence-degree accounting after adjoining right-side derivative data. -> A left point generic over the enlarged field and an identified function field.
- **step-private-valuations** [claim](#stmt-alg-private-valuations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-private-valuations): Construct a private simple zero for each radicand in the plane and every permitted curve case. -> Independent radicand square classes in the enlarged function field.
- **step-algebraic-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-alg-obstruction) / [proof](#pm-h-43): Solve differentiated equations in a finite radical subfield and apply an unused independent sign change. -> Contradiction to the assumed dense multiplicative-rectangle sequence.
- **step-main-power-saving** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-private-valuations-2): Assume failure of every fixed saving, reduce to a sequence and exclude both height-scale alternatives. -> Existence of absolute exponent and constant for every finite cardinality.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-planar-exact-distance-bound**: The maximum number of unordered unit-distance pairs among distinct points in the Euclidean plane admits a uniform bound with an absolute exponent below the classical incidence exponent.
- **invariant-wedge-marginal-preservation**: Sampling a center according to its edge marginal and then independently sampling its point neighbors preserves the point marginal and the uniform edge law in each point–center slot.
- **invariant-fresh-test-prediction**: A strict-prefix history can be selected before the fresh test while retaining the stated average prediction discrepancy bound. The actual endpoints need not become independent after their histories are specified.
- **invariant-complete-rectangle-height**: Retained edge ratios have large individual height, while alternating products on complete rectangles have negligible height. The assigned centers of the rectangle edges may differ.
- **invariant-private-square-class-witnesses**: A valuation that is odd on its assigned radicand and vanishes on the others proves square-class independence in the same function field used for the radical extension.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-proximity-and-tolerance-graphs**: Apply the power-saving bound to proximity graphs or approximate distance matches. -> An inequality or tolerance predicate does not provide the exact unit equations used throughout the argument.
- **anti-operational-constant-sizing**: Use the theorem directly to allocate a concrete buffer or certify an operational running-time limit. -> Existence of an absolute exponent gap does not supply numerical values for the exponent or multiplicative constant.
- **anti-current-test-history-leakage**: Choose or update the prediction history using the test whose acceptance is being assessed. -> The fresh test would no longer be independent of the history construction, invalidating the prediction comparison.
- **anti-arbitrary-posterior-support-transfer**: Transfer concentration of predicted states to the actual point for an arbitrary support selected from histories. -> The transfer argument bounds exceptional sets only for the restricted support types in its hypothesis.
- **anti-probability-only-height-pruning**: Remove a small-probability exceptional set before selecting a height band and assume that negligible height mass was lost. -> Rare outcomes may carry substantial first-moment mass; small exceptional probability alone does not control the lost height.
- **anti-square-classes-before-field-enlargement**: Prove square-class independence over an earlier coefficient field and assume it survives adjoining derivative data. -> The required radical automorphisms depend on square-class independence over the enlarged function field itself.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The target is the global power-saving theorem, whose proof excludes hypothetical asymptotic sequences through a nonprincipal ultrafilter, generic limiting positions, and field derivations. It does not provide a finite constructive procedure or numerical exponent gap. The source states: "The proof excludes every sequence whose unit-distance counts approach the exponent $4/3$, establishing the existence of a fixed positive gap without estimating its size." Finite checks of auxiliary identities would not certify this global existence conclusion.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The global bound supplies an existence guarantee for exact planar distance pairs, but does not supply numerical constants or a procedure for sizing finite software resources. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The counted relation is exact Euclidean unit distance between distinct planar points. Collision volumes, proximity interactions and tolerance bands require different counting guarantees. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Algebraic specialization preserves prescribed distance equations and distinctness, but its proof obtains a solution through a transfer principle without specifying a solver or a computational cost. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The sampled-circle cutting is a finite construction: sample circles, delete points on sampled boundaries, and sweep the resulting semicircle arrangement into open regions with controlled conflict lists. Its geometric procedure can be adapted independently of the nonconstructive final exponent improvement. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The incidence graph and its degree-weighted probability laws organize the proof, but the paper does not give a storage representation, update procedure or data-structure performance contract. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The weighted absolute values range over embeddings and nonarchimedean entries of a configuration-dependent number field. This arithmetic framework supplies no accelerator architecture or bounded arithmetic representation. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Independent square classes and radical sign changes establish an algebraic contradiction. They do not establish computational hardness, secrecy or a cryptographic construction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Prediction means sampling from a finite point marginal conditioned on a fixed test history. The discrepancy guarantee concerns an explicitly specified point–center law and a fresh independent test, without supplying a learning procedure for unknown distributions. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Maximizing a rectangle density is an existence selection over a finite family inside an asymptotic incidence reduction. The paper gives neither an efficient optimizer nor an operational objective with a performance guarantee. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Templates and conditional star experiments preserve mathematical sampling marginals. They provide no communication protocol, consistency rule or distributed execution construction. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The principal contribution is an extremal planar geometry theorem proved through asymptotic height analysis and an algebraic obstruction. It does not construct configurations attaining the upper bound or expose an additional engineering implementation. |
